[26-042] Ch26 · Provincial Dynasty of North India and Deccan Province · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Re. Exam) (Pre) 2015
Q: Who were Polygars of South India?
Options: (a) Ordinary Zamindars (b) Mahajans (c) Territorial Administrative and Military Governors (d) Newly enriched traders
Answer: (c) Territorial Administrative and Military Governors
Explanation: Palaiyakkarar or Polygar was the feudal title of a class of administrative and Military governors appointed by the Nayaka rulers of South India during 16th to 18th centuries.

[27-001] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. U.D.A./L.D.A. (Pre) 2001
Q: The Bhakti movement was started by –
Options: (a) Alvar Saints (b) Sufi Saints (c) Surdas (d) Tulsidas
Answer: (a) Alvar Saints
Explanation: The Bhakti Movement is a Hindu religious movement in which the main spiritual practise is loving devotion among the Shaiva Nayanars and the Vaishnava Alvars. Devotional worship had a long history of almost a thousand years across the sub-continent. The early medieval Bhakti movement originated in Dravida region and began to spread to the north during the late medieval ages (during 13th-14th centuries onwards) when … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[27-002] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 1993
Q: The Bhakti culture was reborn in India during-
Options: (a) Vedic age (b) 10th century AD (c) 12th century AD (d) 15th-16th century AD
Answer: (d) 15th-16th century AD
Explanation: The Bhakti Movement started with the great philosopher Adi Shankaracharya propounding the philosophy of Advaitavada to eliminate the influence of Buddhism during the 8th century. The Bhakti movement was reborn in the 15th-16th century when Kabir, Tulsi, Nanak, Sur, Mirabai etc. encouraged the movement.

[27-003] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): R.A.S./R.T.S. (Pre) 1992
Q: The common belief of life of both Buddha and Mirabai was–
Options: (a) Following ahimsa (b) Austerity of Nirvana (c) The world is full of sorrow (d) Speak of truth
Answer: (c) The world is full of sorrow
Explanation: Buddha and Mirabai had a common philosophy which stated that the world is full of sorrow. 'Sorrow is first among the Four Arya Truths of Buddhism'.

[27-005] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): 47th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2005
Q: A common feature of all Bhakti Saints was that they –
Options: (a) Composed their verses in the language understood by their followers (b) Rejected the authority of the priestly class (c) Encouraged women to go to the temples (d) Encouraged idol worship
Answer: (a) Composed their verses in the language understood by their followers

They also contributed to the advancement of Hindi, Kannada, Punjabi, Bengali, Telugu, Tamil and other languages. The Saints of Bhakti Movement gave their verses in regional and local languages so that their followers could easily understand them. This was the main reason behind the … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[27-006] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2012
Q: With reference to the religious history of medieval India, the Sufi mystics were known to pursue which of the following practices?
   | 1. | Meditation and control of breath. |
   | 2. | Severe ascetic exercises in a lonely place. |
   | 3. | Recitation of holy songs to arouse a state ofecstasy |
   in their audience.
   Select the correct answer using the codes given below:
Options: (a) 1 and 2 (b) 2 and 3 (c) Only 3 (d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d) 1, 2 and 3
Explanation: The emergence of Sufi saints in medieval India helped to promote communal harmony in the society. They promoted activities like meditation and practised spiritual disciplines such as remembrance and devotional practices like 'Sama' and sometimes breath-focused remembrance. They took the help of holy songs and music to spread unity and harmony in society and to arouse a state of spiritual ecstasy in their audience.

[27-007] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2002; U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2015
Q: Vaishnavism in Kamarupa was popularised by –
Options: (a) Chaitanya (b) Nimbarka (c) Ramananda (d) Shankaradeva
Answer: (d) Shankaradeva
Also asked: Famous medieval saint Shankaradeva belonged to – -> Vaishnava Cult
Explanation: Kamarupa was an ancient India state situated in Assam. Vaishnavism in Kamarupa was popularized by Shankaradeva, the founder of Ekasarana sect. They believed in devotion to one deity-Krishna (identified with Vishnu). They also opposed both rituals and idolatry. They were known as Chaitanya of Assam.

[27-009] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2011

[27-010] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1991
Q: Ramanujacharya is related to –
Options: (a) Bhakti (b) Dvaitvad (c) Vishistadvaita (d) Monotheism
Answer: (c) Vishistadvaita
Explanation: Ramanujacharya was the earliest propagator of the Bhakti Movement. The philosophy of Sri Ramanujacharya is known in Sanskrit as Vishishtadvaita. This term literally means “non-duality of reality as characterized by attributes. The central idea of Vishishtadvaita is this: ‘there exists an ultimate principle, an absolute being that is the source and substratum of all that exists.’

[27-011] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.U.D.A./L.D.A. (Pre) 2002
Q: “Shuddha Advaitvad” was propounded by –
Options: (a) Madhvacharya (b) Vallabhacharya (c) Shrikantacharya (d) Ramanuja
Answer: (b) Vallabhacharya
Explanation: Shuddha Advaitavada is the “purely non-dual” philosophy propounded by Shri Vallabhacharya, the founder and guru of the Vallabha Sect. Vallabhacharya was the son of Telugu Brahmin Shri Lakshmana Bhatta of Somyaji clan, who is said to have developed the views of Vishnuswami into Shuddha Advaita or pure non-dualism.

[27-012] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): Chhattisgarh P.C.S. (Pre) 2015
Q: W h e re i s t h e b i r t h p l a c e o f “ M a h a p r a b h u Vallabhacharya”?
Options: (a) Shivarinarayan (b) Bilaspur (c) Ratanpur (d) Champaranya (e) None of the above
Answer: (d) Champaranya
Explanation: Mahaprabhu Vallabhacharya was born in the forest named ‘Champaranya’ (Vikram Samvat 1535). He was a devotional philosopher, who founded the Krishna-centred Pushti sect of Vaishnavism and the philosophy of Suddha Advaita.

[27-016] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): Rajasthan P.C.S (Pre) 2024
Q: Which of the following statements regarding the Bhakti Movement is not correct?
Options: (a) Vallabhacharya established the Pushtimarg. (b) Nimbarka propounded the philosophy of Dvaitadvaita. (c) Madhvacharya propounded Nirguna Bhakti Siddhant instead of Saguna Bhakti. (d) Ramananda advocated Rama Bhakti. (e) Question not attempted
Answer: (c) Madhvacharya propounded Nirguna Bhakti Siddhant instead of Saguna Bhakti.
Explanation: The renowned saint of the Bhakti Movement tradition, Madhvacharya, was a proponent of Dvaita (Dualism) philosophy. His central doctrine is "Dvaitavada", which states that the individual soul (ātman), the material world, and the Supreme Being (Paramatma) are distinct and fundamentally different from each other. Madhvacharya followed the Saguna Bhakti tradition, not the Nirguna Bhakti stream. He considered Lord Vishnu … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[27-017] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2014
Q: Consider the following statements:
   1. ‘Bijak’ is a composition of the teachings of Saint Dadu Dayal.
   2. The Philosophy of Pushti Marg was propounded by Madhvacharya.
   Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
Options: (a) 1 only (b) 2 only (c) Both 1 and 2 (d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d) Neither 1 nor 2
Explanation: Bijak is the best-known compilation of the works of Kabir and it is Holy Scripture for followers of the Kabirpanthi sects. Pushtimarg is a Vaishnava sect of Hinduism, founded by Vallabhacharya. He propounded Shuddha Advaita. Thus, both the statements are incorrect. Hence, option (d) is the correct answer.

[27-018] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2002
Q: Who among the following was the first Bhakti Saint to use Hindi for the propagation of his message?
Options: (a) Dadu (b) Kabir (c) Ramananda (d) Tulsidas
Answer: (c) Ramananda
Explanation: Ramananda was born in 1299 A.D. at Prayag (Prayagraj) in an orthodox Kanyakubja Brahmin family. He got an education in Prayag and Varanasi. Swami Ramananda changed the spiritual landscape of northern India. He propagated the worship of Rama and Sita among the masses.

Answer: (b) Ramanand
Explanation: Ramananda was born in Prayagraj. He was the great saint of North India. Raidas was born in Varanasi. Tulsidas was born in Chitrakoot (Rajapur) and Kumbhandas in Mathura.

[27-030] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2007
Q: Which of the following was the birthplace of Guru Nanak?
Options: (a) Amritsar (b) Nabha (c) Nankana (d) Nanded
Answer: (c) Nankana
Explanation: Nankana Sahib is a city and capital of Nankana Sahib district in the Punjab province of Pakistan. Nankana was the birthplace of Guru Nanak, the founder of Sikh religion. He was born on April 15, 1469 AD, in a Khatri family. He had a strong faith in the worship of Nirakar Brahma. He passed away in 1539 AD at Derababa Nanak (Kartarpur Pakistan).

[27-031] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Spl) (Pre) 2008
Q: In whose regime Guru Nanak Dev founded Sikh religion?
Options: (a) Firuz Shah Tughluq (b) Sikandar Lodi (c) Humayun (d) Akbar
Answer: (b) Sikandar Lodi
Explanation: Guru Nanak(1469-1539 A.D.) founded the Sikh religion during the period of Sikandar Lodi (1489-1517 A.D.). Nanak used to believe in monotheism and emphasised the worship of one formless God (Ik Onkar). He believed that there was one and a shapeless God. He did not believe in anthropomorphism.

[27-032] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): R.A.S./R.T.S.(Pre) 2010

Answer: (c) Nanak
Explanation: Guru Nanak says: "God doesnot ask about social class or birth; you must find your true home." Guru Nanak started free community kitchen by the name of ‘Guru Ka Langar.’ His followers used to take food together without any caste discrimination.

[27-039] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2013
Q: Consider the following Bhakti Saints
   1. Dadu Dayal
   2. Guru Nanak
   3. Tyagaraja
   Who among the above was/were preaching when the Lodi dynasty fell and Babur took over?
Options: (a) 1 and 3 (b) Only 2 (c) 2 and 3 (d) 1 and 2
Answer: (b) Only 2
Explanation: Dadu Dayal lived between 1544 to 1603 A.D. Guru Nanak lived between 1469 and 1539 A.D. and Tyagaraja between 1767 to 1847 A.D.. Tyagaraja was the devotee poet of Bhakti path and a great musician of Carnatic music. The collapse of Lodi dynasty started in 1526A.D. after Babur defeated Ibrahim Lodi in the first Battle of Panipat. Guru Nanak delivered his preachings at that time. Thus, option (b) is the correct answer.

Options: (a) Akbar and Jahangir (b) Shahjahan (c) Aurangzeb (d) Babur and Humayun
Answer: (a) Akbar and Jahangir
Explanation: The famous devotee/saint Goswami Tulsidas was the contemporary of Akbar and Jahangir. Although Humayun, Akbar and Jahangir were Mughal emperors during the lifetime of Tulsidas. He wrote, ‘Ramcharitmanas’ and ‘Vinay Patrika’ are best among all of them. Tulsidas had no relation with the court. He spent most of his time in Banaras (Varanasi). He is mentioned in Ain-i-Akbari of Abul Fazl.

Explanation: The “Chishtia Sufi Order” was established in Chishti Sharif of Afghanistan by Abu Ishaq Shami Chishti and his disciple Khwaja Abu Ahmad Abdal, but it was primarily established in India by Muinuddin Chishti. [27-064] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1997
Q: Given below are two statements, one labelled as Assertion (A) and the other labelled as Reason (R).
   Assertion (A): The sponsor and the most prominent figure of the Chishti order of Sufis in India is Khwaja Muinuddin Chishti.
   Reason (R): The Chishti order takes its name from a village Chishti in Ajmer.
   In the context of the above two statements, which one of the following is correct?
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) is individually true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A). (b) Both (A) and (R) is individually true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A). (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false. (d) (A) is false, but (R) is true.
Answer: (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false.
Explanation: The Chishti order was founded by Khwaja Abu Ishaq Shami of Syria, but it was introduced in India by Khwaja Muinuddin Chishti, who came along with Muhammad Ghori army in 1192 AD. Khwaja Abu Ishaq Sami migrated from Syria to Chist (Chishti Sharif, Afghanistan), hence, was called Chishti. He was 9th generation descendant of Hazrat Ali. Therefore, assertion (A) is right, but reason (R) is wrong.

[27-066] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): R.A.S./R.T.S. (Pre) 2016
Q: Who among the following does not belong to the Chishti order?

Answer: (b) Shaikh Abdul Jilani
Explanation: Chishti order is a Sunni Sufi order within the mystic Sufi tradition of Islam. Muinuddin Chishti introduced the Chishti order in India. Qutbuddin Bakhtiyar Kaki was a saint of Chishti order from Delhi; Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya was a saint of Chishti order in Indian sub-continent. Shaikh Abdul Jilani did not belong to Chishti Silsila. Rather, he founded Qadiri order.

[27-085] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): Jharkhand P.C.S. (Pre) 2011
Q: From the given pairs identify the incorrect pair –
Options: (a) Chishti-Delhi and Doab (b) Suhrawardi-Sindh (c) Auliya-Madhya Pradesh (d) Firdausi-Bihar
Answer: (c) Auliya-Madhya Pradesh
Explanation: The dominion of Chishti order existed in Delhi and surrounding areas while the area of Suhrawardi order existed in Sindh region. Firdausi order was a part of Chishti order whose dominion existed in the region of Bihar. Auliya is not any order, but a title given to ‘Nizamuddin’ the famous Saint of Delhi. The dominion of Nizamuddin Auliya existed in Delhi. Thus, option (c) is incorrect.

[27-086] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): 70th B.P.S.C. Re-Exam 2024
Q: Which of the following Sufi Silsilah was founded in Bihar?
Options: (a) Quaderia (b) Firdousia (c) Qalandhar (d) Kubriya
Answer: (b) Firdousia
Explanation: The Firdausia (Firdousi) Silsila was established in Bihar. In India, the most prominent Sufi associated with this Silsila was Sheikh Sharfuddin Yahya Maneri. Sheikh Maneri was the key propagator of this Silsila in Bihar.

[27-087] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 3 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2012; U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2003; U.P.U.D.A./L.D.A. (Pre) 2002
Q: Which of the following Sufis regarded Krishna among the Auliyas:
Options: (a) Shah Muhammad Ghaus (b) Shah Abdul Aziz (c) Shah Waliullah (d) Khwaja Mir Dard
Answer: (a) Shah Muhammad Ghaus
Explanation: The Sufi saint Shah Muhammad Ghaus accepted Lord Krishna as Auliya/Wali. He was the most famous saint of Shattari order. He had a close relationship with Mughal emperor Humayun and Tansen. He expressed his spiritual quest in ‘Jawahir-e-Khamsa’. He translated ‘Amritkund’ of Hatha yoga by the name of‘Bahar-ul-Hayat.’ Shattari Saints tried to bring about reconciliation between Muslims and Hindus by showing equality in … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[27-088] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2018
Q: Shaikh Bahauddin Zakaria belonged to which sect?
Options: (a) Suhrawardi order (b) Rishi order (c) Chishti order (d) Firdausi order (e) None of the above/More than one of the above
Answer: (a) Suhrawardi order
Explanation: Shaikh Bahauddin Zakaria started Suhrawardi order in India. Shaikh Sadr-ud-din Arif, Shaikh Rakh-ud-din Abul Fateh and Shaikh Jalaluddin Surkh were associated with this Sufi sect.

[27-089] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): Raj. P.C.S. (Pre) 2023
Q: The spread of which of the following Sufi sect was mostly limited to Sindh, Multan and Punjab?
Options: (a) Qadiri (b) Naqshbandi (c) Suhrawardi (d) Chishti (e) Question not attempted
Answer: (c) Suhrawardi
Explanation: The Suhrawardi Sufi Sect entered India at about the same time as Chishtis sect but its activities were confined largely to the Punjab, Multan and Sindh. This Sufi Sect was established in India by Baha-Ud-Din Zakariya.

[27-090] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.S.C. (R.I.) 2014
Q: Which of the following is not related to Sufism?
Options: (a) Ulema (b) Khanqah (c) Shaikh (d) Sama
Answer: (a) Ulema
Explanation: ‘Khanqah’ was the place of residence of Sufi saints. ‘Sama’ was a function organised by Sufis. ‘Shaikh’ was the authorized person who provided education and guidance in Sufism. Ulemas were scholarly persons to know the religious laws of Islam. Therefore, Ulema is not related to Sufism.

[27-091] Ch27 · Bhakti and Sufi Movement · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1996
Q: Prem Vatika, poems on the life of Krishna, was composed by:
Options: (a) Bihari (b) Surdas (c) Raskhan (d) Kabir
Answer: (c) Raskhan
Explanation: Raskhan was a poet and a follower of Lord Krishna. His real name was Sayyid Ibrahim. ‘Prem Vatika’ is a poetry text written in Braj language by Raskhan. He depicted the life of Krishna and the ideal of love/devotion in this text. ‘Sujan Raskhan’ is also one of the famous texts written by Raskhan.

[28-003] Ch28 · Mughal Dynasty:Babur · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. U.D.A./L.D.A. (Pre) 2010
Q: The Mughal rulers of medieval India were –
Options: (a) Persian (Iranian) (b) Afghans (c) Chagatai Turk (d) None of the above
Answer: (c) Chagatai Turk
Explanation: The Mughal rulers belonged to the Chagatai branch of Turks. This name was derived after the second son of Genghis Khan. He was the ruler of central Asia and Turkistan.

[28-004] Ch28 · Mughal Dynasty:Babur · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. Lower Sub. (Pre) 2015
Q: Who among the following defeated Babur in the Battle of Sar-e-Pul?
Options: (a) Abdullah Khan Uzbeg (b) Shaybani Khan (c) Ubaydullah Khan (d) Jani Begh
Answer: (b) Shaybani Khan
Explanation: Shaybani Khan invaded Ferghana in 1501 A.D., defeated Babur at the Sar-e-Pul and occupied Samarqand. Babur fled and, for three years, wandered around the hills of Afghanistan and the plains of Khurasan. He made several attempts to recapture Samarkand, but each time, he suffered defeat at the hands of Shaybani Khan. Tulughuma technique was used by Shaybani to defeat Babur.

[28-005] Ch28 · Mughal Dynasty:Babur · asked in 1 paper(s): Jharkhand P.S.C. (Mains) 2016
Q: Which Mughal emperor followed Ottoman system of war in India?
Options: (a) Akbar (b) Shah Jahan (c) Babur (d) None of the above
Answer: (c) Babur
Explanation: Mughal ruler Babur used Ottoman system of war during the first Battle of Panipat (1526 AD). The most important reason for the victory of Babur in the battle was his artillery canons guns led by Ali Quli and use of Tulughma and Araba tactics by him.

[28-009] Ch28 · Mughal Dynasty:Babur · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1996; M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2005
Q: At which of the following battles was artillery used for the first time by one of the two armies?
Options: (a) First Battle of Panipat (b) Battle of Khanwa (c) Battle of Plassey (d) Third Battle of Panipat
Answer: (a) First Battle of Panipat
Also asked: Whom did Babur defeat in the first battle of Panipat? -> Ibrahim Lodi
Explanation: In India, artillery was used for the first time in the Battle of Panipat. But Babur mentioned the use of artillery and gun for the first time in the Battle of Bajaur (Pakistan) in 1519 A.D. The first battle of Panipat was fought on April 21, 1526 AD. It was fought between Babur and Ibrahim Lodi. The battalion of artillery was headed by Ustad Ali Quli, and the battalion of gunners was headed by Mustafa.

[28-020] Ch28 · Mughal Dynasty:Babur · asked in 1 paper(s): Chhattisgarh P.C.S. (Pre) 2003
Q: After becoming the Mughal Ruler in India, Zahiruddin Muhammad adopted the name:
Options: (a) Babur (b) Humayun (c) Jahangir (d) Bahadur Shah
Answer: (a) Babur
Explanation: The full name of'Babur' was Zahiruddin Muhammad Babur. He was born on February 14, 1483 AD, to Umar Shaikh Mirza and Qutlugh Nigar Khanum at Ferghana. After the death of his father, he ascended the throne of Ferghana (now in Uzbekistan) in June 1494 AD, as an 11 years and four months old minor. He declared himself the ruler on April 27, 1526 AD and founded Mughal empire in India.

[28-021] Ch28 · Mughal Dynasty:Babur · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2015
Q: Babur adopted the title of ‘Padshah’ first at –
Options: (a) Ferghana (b) Kabul (c) Delhi (d) Samarqand
Answer: (b) Kabul
Explanation: Babur captured Samarkand during his reign in 1501 A.D., which lasted only for eight months. After losing Samarkand and Ferghana, Babur moved towards Kabul. After the victory of Kabul in 1504, he gave up the title ‘Mirza’ held by his ancestors and adopted the title of ‘Padshah.’

[28-025] Ch28 · Mughal Dynasty:Babur · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1993
Q: Given below are two statements, one is labelled Assertion (A), and other is labelled as Reason (R).
   Assertion (A): Babur wrote his memoirs in Turki.
   Reason (R): Turki was the official language of the Mughal Court.
   In the context of the above two statements, which one of the following is correct?
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A). (b) Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not a correct explanation of (A). (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false. (d) (A) is false, but (R) is true.
Answer: (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false.
Explanation: The founder of the Mughal Empire, Zahiruddin Muhammad Babur wrote his memoirs (autobiography) ‘Tuzuk-i-Babri’ also known as Baburnama written in Turkish language containing related events of his life. The Turkish language was not the official language of the Mughal Empire. The Persian language was the official language of Mughal empire. Thus, Assertion (A) is correct, but Reason (R) is incorrect.

[29-017] Ch29 · Humayun and Sher Shah · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. R.O./A.R.O. (Mains) 2021
Q: With reference to Sher Shah Suri, which of the following statements is/are correct?
   1. He was the architect of a brilliant administrative system.
   2. However his qualities as a ruler were not more remarkable than his victories on the field of battle.
   Select the correct answer from the code given below:
   Code:
Options: (a) Only 1 (b) Only 2 (c) Both 1 and 2 (d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a) Only 1
Explanation: A brave warrior and a successful conqueror, Sher Shah was the architect of a brilliant administrative system. In fact, his qualities as a ruler were more remarkable than his victories on the battlefields. His brief reign of five years was marked by the introduction of wise and salutary changes in every conceivable branch of administration. Hence, the first statement is true, while the second statement is false.

[29-018] Ch29 · Humayun and Sher Shah · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 1993; U.P.R.O./A.R.O. (Mains) 2016

Options: (a) Agra (b) Kalinjar (c) Rohtas (d) Sasaram
Answer: (b) Kalinjar
Also asked: Last expedition of Sher Shah Suri was against which of the following States? -> Kalanjar
Explanation: Sher Shah Suri died on May 22, 1545 AD, during the siege of the Kalinjar Fort of Rajputs. When all the tactics to subdue this fort failed, he ordered the walls of the fort to be blown up with gunpowder. He was seriously wounded by the explosion. The Kalinjar was last campaign of Shershah.

[29-021] Ch29 · Humayun and Sher Shah · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2022

Answer: (d) Marwar

Due to the valour and courage of Jaita and Kumpa, Sher Shah Suri’s army suffered heavy losses. Sher Shah is said to have commented that “for a few grains of bajra (Millets), I almost lost the entire kingdom of Hindustan”.

Answer: (c) Sher Shah
Explanation: Sher Shah was a medieval ruler who introduced the system of‘’patta’’ and ‘’qabuliyat’’ to help the peasants.The peasants were given ‘’patta’’ by the central authority, in which the share of the government was clearly mentioned. The peasants accepted it through ‘’qabuliyat’’.

[30-021] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2009
Q: Who among the following emperors may be called “an enlightened despot”?
Options: (a) Babur (b) Humayun (c) Akbar (d) Aurangzeb
Answer: (c) Akbar
Explanation: Akbar was a great ruler whose empire extended from Bengal to Afghanistan in the north and from Kashmir to the Godavari in the south. He was a liberal and tolerant emperor, and the complete authority of the empire was vested in him. Therefore, he is considered as ''an enlightened despot'' like Queen Elizabeth I of England and Frederick the Great, during whose reign all subjects were equal before the law.

[30-023] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2002
Q: Who among the following had ordered that a man should marry only one wife and could marry another only when the first wife was barren?
Options: (a) Alauddin Khalji (b) Sher Shah (c) Akbar (d) None of the above
Answer: (c) Akbar
Explanation: Akbar banned some social customs and personal opinions, but he did that to (or “intended to”) improve the society. For example, he ordered that a man could marry only one woman and could marry another only when the first wife proved barren. He also tried to promote the social reforms by encouraging widow remarriage and by discouraging “Sati’ practice forcefully.

[30-027] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1992

Answer: (a) Mansabdari

After this action, he organised soldiers by Mansabdari system in 1574-75 A.D. Mansab means rank or position. … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[30-030] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1994
Q: The Mansabdari System introduced by Akbar was borrowed from the system followed in –
Options: (a) Afghanistan (b) Turkey (c) Mongolia (d) Persia
Answer: (c) Mongolia

[30-031] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1999
Q: Assertion (A): During the time of Akbar, for every ten cavalrymen, the mansabdars had to maintain twenty horses.
   Reason (R): Horses had to be rested while on march and replacements were necessary in times of war.
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) is true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A). (b) Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not a correct explanation of (A). (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false. (d) (A) is false, but (R) is true.
Answer: (a) Both (A) and (R) is true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A).
Explanation: During the reign of Akbar, for every ten cavalrymen, the Mansabdars had to maintain twenty horses. The main reason for this custom was that it was essential to give rest to the horses during the longjourney and it was needed to replace horses during the war. Thus, Assertion (A) and Reason (R) both are correct, and Reason (R) is the correct explanation of Assertion (A).

[30-032] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): 39th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 1994
Q: Zabti system was introduced by –
Options: (a) Ghiyasuddin Tughluq (b) Sikandar Lodi (c) Sher Shah (d) Akbar
Answer: (d) Akbar
Explanation: Zabti System was introduced for land revenue in the reign of Akbar which was measurement based assessment; revenue circles (dasturs) with per-bigha cash rates; Ain-i-Dahsala (ten year average rate). It was based on schedules of Dastur ul-Amal and Zabti Khasre for land survey and land revenue determination. Most of the area was based on this system.

[30-035] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2012

Options: (a) Law (b) Land Revenue Reforms (c) Literature (d) Music
Answer: (b) Land Revenue Reforms

He calculated the average produce of different crops as well as average price prevailing over the last ten years from 1571 to 1580 AD. [30-039] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 3 paper(s): Chhattisgarh P.C.S. (Pre) 2011; M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1998; 66th B.P.S.C. Re-Exam (Pre) 2020

Answer: (d) 1582
Also asked: Which ruler had promoted ‘’Din-i-Ilahi? -> Akbar
Also asked: Who promulgated Din-i-Ilahi? -> Akbar
Explanation: Akbar launched "Tauhid-i-Ilahi" or "Din-i-Ilahi" in 1582 AD. Din-i-Ilahi was a syncretic religion which intended to merge the best elements of all the religions of the community. Indeed, the prophet of this new sect was Abul Fazl. Among Hindus, only Birbal accepted Din-i-Ilahi. Raja Bhagawan Das and Man Singh bluntly refused to embrace Din-i-Ilahi.

[30-042] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2000

Answer: (d) None of these
Explanation: "Din-i-Ilahi" was not a religion but an ideology based on Sufi pantheism. Jahangir’s contemporary Mohsin Fani mentioned Din-i-Ilahi as an independent religion in his text ‘’Dabistan i-Mazahib’’.

[30-065] Ch30 · Akbar · asked in 1 paper(s): R.A.S./R.T.S.(Pre) 1999
Q: The translation of the Mahabharat into Persian in the time of Akbar was carried out under the supervision of:
Options: (a) Utbi (b) Naziri (c) Abul Fazl (d) Faizi
Answer: (d) Faizi
Explanation: Akbar established Translation Department under the supervision of his poet laureate Abul Fazl. The translation of the Mahabharat into Persian at the time of Akbar was carried out under the direction of Faizi by the concerted effort of Naqib Khan, Badauni, Faizi etc.

[31-001] Ch31 · Jahangir · asked in 1 paper(s): 46th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2004
Q: Who introduced ‘Do-aspa and Sih-aspa’ system?
Options: (a) Akbar (b) Jahangir (c) Shah Jahan (d) Aurangzeb
Answer: (b) Jahangir
Explanation: ‘Do-aspa and Sih-aspa’ system was introduced by Jahangir. Under the provision of this system, Mansabdars had to maintain an excess number of soldiers without increment in rank (zat). / Do-aspa: In this system, Mansabdars had to maintain double horses in comparison to their ‘Sawar’ rank. / Sih-aspa: In this system, Mansabdars had to maintain a triple number of horses in comparison to their ‘Sawar’ rank.

Explanation: It was the reign of Jahangir in which the ‘’Treaty of Chittor’’ was signed between Mughals and Rana of Mewar in 1615 A.D. Rana accepted the allegiance of Mughal emperor and Jahangir returned Mewar and Chittorgarh to Rana which were annexed by Mughals during the reign of Akbar.

Explanation: The tomb of Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya is in Delhi, not Ajmer. He died in 1325 AD and was buried at Ghiyaspur (Delhi). Hence, option (d) is not the correct answer. Amir Khusrau and Hasan Muhammad Dehlavi were eminent disciples of Auliya. Other pairs are correctly matched.

[32-002] Ch32 · Shah Jahan · asked in 1 paper(s): 39th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 1994
Q: What was the root of the dispute between the Shah of Iran and Mughal rulers?
Options: (a) Kabul (b) Qandahar (c) Kunduz (d) Ghazni
Answer: (b) Qandahar
Explanation: Qandahar was the cause of dispute between the Shah of Iran and the Mughal rulers of Qandahar because of strategic and economic grounds and a prestigious issue for Persians and Mughals. The expansion of colonialism of both the dynasties was mostly depended on suzerainty of Qandahar.

[32-003] Ch32 · Shah Jahan · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1998
Q: The loss of Qandahar was a big blow to the Mughal empire from the viewpoint of:
Options: (a) Natural resources (b) Buffer territory (c) Communication (d) Strategic stronghold
Answer: (d) Strategic stronghold
Explanation: The loss of Qandahar was strategically a big blow to the Mughal empire. The loss of Qandahar exposed the Mughal Kingdom to outside attacks. Finally, Qandahar slipped out from the hands of the Mughals during the period of Shah Jahan.

[32-006] Ch32 · Shah Jahan · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2002
Q: The motive behind Shah Jahan’s Balkh campaign was to:
Options: (a) Secure a friendly ruler in Balkh and Badakshan which bordered Kabul (b) Conquer Samarqand and Ferghana, the Mughal homelands (c) Fix the Mughal frontier on the ‘scientific line’the Amu Daria (d) Expand the Mughal Empire beyond the sub-continent
Answer: (a) Secure a friendly ruler in Balkh and Badakshan which bordered Kabul
Explanation: The motive behind Shah Jahan’s Balkh and Badakshan campaign was not to conquer Samarqand and Ferghana, the Mughal homelands but to remove the Persian representative and secure a friendly ruler in Balkh and Badakshan which bordered Kabul. This would help him in controlling the rebel Afghan tribes living near Ghazni and Khyber. So, it became a buffer state between Mughals and Persians.

[33-001] Ch33 · Aurangzeb · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1994
Q: Assertion(A): Aurangzeb succeeded Shah Jahan to the Mughal throne.
   Reason (R): The law ofprimogeniture was followed.
   Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A). (b) Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A). (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false. (d) (A) is false, but (R) is true.
Answer: (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false.
Explanation: Aurangzeb succeeded Shah Jahan to the Mughal throne, but the rule of primogeniture was not followed where the eldest son inherited his father’s estate. He succeeded the throne by force. There were no clear rules of succession; it was the subject of power. Dara was the eldest and Aurangzeb was the third son of Shah Jahan. So, Assertion (A) is correct, but Reason (R) is not correct.

[33-002] Ch33 · Aurangzeb · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2009; U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2006
Q: Who of the following Mughal emperor was coronated twice?
Options: (a) Akbar (b) Jahangir (c) Shah Jahan (d) Aurangzeb
Answer: (d) Aurangzeb
Explanation: The coronation of the Mughal emperor Aurangzeb occurred twice. His first coronation was held on 21 July, 1658 in Delhi and second was held on 5 June, 1659 on the success of Khajwa and Deoria battle.

Answer: (c) Aurangzeb and Dara Shikoh
Explanation: Battle of Dharmat (Madhya Pradesh) was fought between Aurangzeb and forces of Dara Shikoh for their father Shah Jahan’s throne. Dara Shikoh was supported by Raja Jaswant Singh, and Aurangzeb was supported by Prince Murad. On 15th April, 1658 Aurangzeb became victorious.

Answer: (b) Aurangzeb

The Hindus were categorised into three classes for the purpose of paying tax: / (i) Those with income less than 200 Dirham per annum, had to pay 12 Dirham per annum as tax. / (ii) Those with income 200 to 10,000 Dirham per annum, had to pay 24 Dirham per annum as tax. / (iii) Those with income more than 10,000 Dirham per … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[33-021] Ch33 · Aurangzeb · asked in 1 paper(s): 43rd B.P.S.C. (Pre) 1999
Q: The meaning of ‘Jihad’ carried on by Aurangzeb is –
Options: (a) Darul-Harb (b) Dar-ul-Islam (c) Holy War (d) Jizya
Answer: (b) Dar-ul-Islam
Explanation: Aurangzeb was a Sunni Muslim. Aurangzeb tried to enforce the Quranic law, according to which it enjoins upon every pious Muslim to carry on Jihad (holy wars) against non Muslim lands (Dar-ul-Harb) till they are converted to Islam (Dar-ul-Islam).

[33-022] Ch33 · Aurangzeb · asked in 3 paper(s): R.A.S./R.T.S. (Pre) 1999; U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2013; U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2000
Q: Creator of "Bibi Ka Maqbara/ Tomb of Bibi" was –
Options: (a) Humayun (b) Shah Jahan (c) Akbar II (d) Aurangzeb
Answer: (d) Aurangzeb
Also asked: Which one of the following tombs is called the ‘Second Taj Mahal’: -> Tomb of Rabia-ud-Daurani
Explanation: Aurangzeb's son Azam Shah (during Aurangzeb's reign) constructed "Bibi Ka Maqbara" for his mother Rabia-ud Daurani which is an architectural wonder with intricate designs, carved motifs, imposing structures and beautifully landscaped Mughal style garden. It is known as 'Tomb of Bibi.” It is called ‘The Mini Taj’ or Deccan ‘Taj Mahal.’ It is also known as the tomb of Rabia-ud-Daurani.

[34-001] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 2 paper(s): Uttarakhand P.C.S. (Pre) 2004; U.P. Lower Sub. (Pre) 2009
Q: What was ‘district’ known as during the Mughal administration?
Options: (a) Ahar (b) Vishyas (c) Suba (d) Sarkar
Answer: (d) Sarkar
Also asked: What was the district called during the Mughal period? -> Sarkar
Explanation: Akbar divided the empire into units for the convenience of administration. Entire empire was divided into Subas (provinces). These were further divided into Sarkars (districts) and Parganas (sub-districts). Several villages constituted a Paragana. Every Sarkar had a chiefofficer like Faujdar, Amalguzar, Qazi, Kotwal, Bitiqhi and Khajandar.

[34-003] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1992

Options: (a) Shane-e-Peel (b) Mir Bakshi (c) Wazir (d) Sawahenigar
Answer: (b) Mir Bakshi
Explanation: During the Mughal period, the head of the military was called Mir Bakshi, who was appointed by the leading nobles of the court. Mir Bakshi was in charge of intelligence gathering and also made recommendations to the emperor for military appointments and promotions.

[34-004] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Spl) (Pre) 2004

Options: (a) Charged Tax from farmers (b) Maintaining accounts of income and Expenses (c) Justice (d) Supervisions of land revenue officials
Answer: (d) Supervisions of land revenue officials
Explanation: During the Mughal period, the duty of Mir Bakshi was to supervise the land revenue officials in addition to the salaries of the military department. Sir Jadunath Sarkar called Mir Bakshi as Pay Master. But the head of land-revenue administration was Wazir/Diwan.

[34-007] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1998

Answer: (b) 1, 2 and 3 are correct

[34-009] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1996
Q: In medieval India, Mansabdari system was introduced mainly for:
Options: (a) Making recruitment to the army (b) Facilitating revenue collection (c) Ensuring religious harmony (d) Effecting clean administration
Answer: (d) Effecting clean administration
Explanation: Akbar introduced a well-organised system in the military and civil service of the empire which came to be known as Mansabdari system. Very soon, this system became the main basis of the military and civil administration of the Mughals. The main intention to introduce Mansabdari was to provide a clean and better administration.

The word 'Mansab' is of Arabic origin meaning rank or position. Hence Mansabdari was a system of ranking the government officials and determining their civil & military duties, along with their renumeration. [34-014] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1995
Q: What was the principal source of State income in Mughal India:
Options: (a) Booty (b) Escheat (c) Land Revenue (d) Tax
Answer: (c) Land Revenue
Explanation: The central feature of the agrarian system under the Mughals was the alienation from the peasant of his surplus produce (produce over and above the subsistence level) in the form of land revenue which was the primary source of state’s income.

Options: (a) Akbar (b) Babur (c) Jahangir (d) Aurangzeb
Answer: (c) Jahangir
Explanation: Portuguese introduced tobacco in India. Soon it became popular among the common man. The Mughal Emperor Jahangir noticed the harmful effects of tobacco and issued an order to ban in 1617 A.D.

Explanation: Sometimes, the Mughal administration issued grants as a Madad-i-Maash to pious or otherwise worthy recipients as charity. The spirit of composite culture and harmony expressed in revenue-free grants (Madad-i-Maash), which the nobles extended to the support of saints, maintenance of religious institutions, religious places and upkeep of Mashaikhs, etc.

[34-024] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2019
Q: Given below are two statements, one labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R).

   Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
   Code:

As a result, during the rule of Akbar and his successors in the Mughal period, gold coins, silver rupees and Copper Dams were extensively circulated and regulated for trading activities. Even during Akbar's time, major copper coin was Dam.

Answer: (b) Daam
Explanation: In the standard Mughal currency system, there were three basic coins: the Mohur of gold, the Rupee of silver and the Dam of copper.

[34-028] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): Jharkhand P.C.S. (Pre) 2023
Q: During the Mughal period, the coins minted during the previous reign were called
Options: (a) Khajana (b) Chalni (c) Mohur (d) Bikha
Answer: (a) Khajana
Explanation: The newly minted coin in the current or previous year was called Taza sikka (newly minted). The coins issued and in circulation in the reign of an emperor were called chalani (current), while the coins minted in the earlier reigns were called Khazana.

[34-029] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): 48th to 52nd B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2008
Q: The meaning of word Bantai during the Medieval period was?
Options: (a) Religious Tax (b) System of calculating revenue (c) Wealth tax (d) Property tax
Answer: (b) System of calculating revenue
Explanation: The meaning of word ‘Batai’ during the medieval period was a system of crop sharing in which real produce was distributed between state and peasants. Sher Shah Suri opted 3 systems for calculating revenue: / Nasq/Muktai/Kankut / Nakadi or Jabti / Galla Bakshi or Bantai / Bantai was of 3 types (i) Khet Bantai (ii) Lank Bantai (iii) Raasi Bantai.

[34-030] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2017
Q: Which of the following systems were prevailing basis of land revenue collection in the Deccan during the reign of Akbar?
Options: (a) Kankut (b) Number of plows (c) Zabt (d) Gallabakshi
Answer: (b) Number of plows
Explanation: In J.N. Sarkar's book 'History of Aurangzeb', it has been mentioned on page number 190, that in North India during the reign of Akbar systematic method of land revenue collection was established by Dewan Todarmal, but this type of systematic method was not found in the Deccan. During this period the farmers of the Deccan used to pay land revenue to the state on per plough (number of plough) basis.

[34-031] Ch34 · Mughal Administration · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2020

Answer: (b) Shahab Nahr
Explanation: Mughal era Shahab (Shihab) Canal was constructed by reinvigorating Firuz Shah's canal Rajabwah. In the time of Akbar, first Shihabuddin Khan & later Nuruddin Muhammad tar reconstructed the canal. During the time of Akbar, Governor of Delhi, Sihabuddin Khan, repaired the canal for irrigation purposes & changed its name to Shahab Canal.

[35-002] Ch35 · Music andPaintingsof Mughal Period · asked in 3 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2012; U.P. U.D.A./L.D.A. (Mains) 2010; U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2009
Q: The foundation of the Mughal painting was laid by –
Options: (a) Akbar (b) Humayun (c) Jahangir (d) Shah Jahan
Answer: (b) Humayun
Explanation: Humayun laid the foundation of Mughal painting during the years of his exile in Persia and Afghanistan. He met two distinguished Painters, Khwaja Abdus Samad and Mir Sayyid Ali. It was from these artists that Humayun and his son Akbar took lessons in drawing. An advanced atelier was set up in the palace.

[35-003] Ch35 · Music andPaintingsof Mughal Period · asked in 1 paper(s): 66th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2020
Q: Which Mughal ruler established Karkhanas for painting?
Options: (a) Humayun (b) Akbar (c) Jahangir (d) Shah Jahan (e) None of the above/More than one of the above
Answer: (b) Akbar
Explanation: Akbar was very fond of painting and during his reign, painting was organised as an imperial establishment or Karkhana called Tasvir Khana where the painters worked. Akbar established Karkhanas for paintings in Agra from 1558 to 1572 A.D., in Fatehpur Sikri from 1572 to 1585 A,D., in Lahore from 1585 to 1601 A.D. and again in Agra from 1603 to 1605 A.D. So, option (b) is the correct option.

[35-004] Ch35 · Music andPaintingsof Mughal Period · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 1995
Q: The Mughal School of Painting formed the spinal cord of the various Schools of Indian miniature art. Which one of the following painting styles was not affected by Mughal painting?
Options: (a) Pahari (b) Rajasthani (c) Kangra (d) Kalighata
Answer: (d) Kalighata
Explanation: The Mughal schools of painting from the Mughal court spread to various regional centres and gave rise to Pahari, Rajasthani school of painting but Kalighata school of painting was Indigenous and not affected by Mughal painting.

[35-005] Ch35 · Music andPaintingsof Mughal Period · asked in 1 paper(s): 46th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2004

Answer: (a) Abdus Samad; (c) Mir Sayyid Ali
Explanation: ) / Most of the Mughals were renowned as great patrons of arts. The first endeavour was to complete the pictures of the earliest Mughal illustrated manuscript, the Dastan-i-Amir Hamza began in the period of Humayun. It took 15 years to finish under the supervision of Mir Sayyid Ali and Abdus Samad and around 100 artists completed it.

[35-006] Ch35 · Music andPaintingsof Mughal Period · asked in 1 paper(s): 60th to 62nd B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2016

[35-007] Ch35 · Music andPaintingsof Mughal Period · asked in 1 paper(s): 63rd B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2017
Q: European paintings were introduced in the court of:
Options: (a) Humayun (b) Akbar (c) Jahangir (d) Shah Jahan (e) None of the above/More than one of the above
Answer: (b) Akbar
Explanation: European paintings were introduced in the court of Akbar through Portuguese. Mughal painters received two specialities from them - / 1. Portrait of person / 2. To make objects seen further in the scene small in size. Development of painting started in Humayun period. European influence on Mughal paintings started during Akbar. Mishkin was an European painter in Akbar Court.

Answer: (a) Todi

[35-017] Ch35 · Music andPaintingsof Mughal Period · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2009

Options: (a) 5 (b) 4 (c) 3 (d) 2
Answer: (a) 5
Explanation: Swami Haridas was a spiritual poet and classical musician credited with a large body of devotional compositions, especially in the Dhrupad style. He was also the founder of Haridasi school ofmysticism, still found today in North India. His followers built 5 worship training centres (ashrams). These are- Sri Banke Bihariji, Nidhivan, Temple of Gorelal, Sri Rasik Bihari and Thatti Khana.

[35-022] Ch35 · Music andPaintingsof Mughal Period · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2013

Explanation: Abdul Hamid Lahori was an official historian during the reign of Shah Jahan. He wrote the book ‘Padshahnama’, also known as ‘Badshahnama,’ the history of Shah Jahan. He joined the court of Shah Jahan as a traveller.

Answer: (d) Ain-e-Akbari

Options: (a) A Persian script used in medieval India (b) A raga composed by Tansen (c) A cess levied by the Mughal rulers (d) A manual of code of conduct for the Ulemas
Answer: (a) A Persian script used in medieval India
Explanation: Nastaliq was a Persian script used in medieval India during the Mughal period. The Mughal Emperor Aurangzeb was an expert in writing Nastaliq script.

[36-030] Ch36 · Mughal Literature · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P.P.S.C. (GIC) 2010; U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2004
Q: The poet Heart King, who wrote praising verses for Lord Krishna under the name of Nagri Das was –
Options: (a) Raja Ummed Singh (b) Raja Ram Singh (c) Raja Chhatrasal (d) Raja Sawant Singh
Answer: (d) Raja Sawant Singh
Explanation: Nagri Das (Servant of Radha) was the nickname of Raja Sawant Singh (17th-18th century) of Kishangarh state, Ajmer. He wrote many verses in praise of Lord Krishna. The rulers of Kishangarh patronized the Nimbarka sect.

Options: (a) Keshav (b) Matiram (c) Raskhan (d) Senapati
Answer: (a) Keshav
Explanation: ‘Ramachandrika’and ‘Rasik-Priya’was written by Keshavdas (1555-1617 A.D.). He was a Sanskrit scholar and Hindi poet, best known for his ‘Rasik Priya’ a pioneering work of the Ritikaal of Hindi literature.

[36-033] Ch36 · Mughal Literature · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2025

Options: (a) Ramachandrika (b) Kavipriya (c) Ratan Bavani (d) Ram Rasayan
Answer: (d) Ram Rasayan
Explanation: The major works of Keshavdas include Ramachandrika, Rasik Priya, Ratan Bavani, Kavipriya, Chhandamala, etc. Ram Rasayan, Ganga Lahari and Prabodh Pachasa etc. are works written by Padmakar.

Q: Select the correct chronological order for the following Muslim rulers from the codes given below the names:
   | 1. Ahmad Shah Abdali | 2. Muhammad Shah |
   | 3. Jahangir | 4. Bahadur Shah II |
   Code:
Options: (a) 1, 2, 3, 4 (b) 4, 3, 2, 1 (c) 3, 2, 1, 4 (d) 2, 1, 3, 4
Answer: (c) 3, 2, 1, 4
Explanation: The correct chronological order of the above-mentioned Muslim rulers is as follows. Jahangir ruled from 1605 to 1627 A.D. Muhammad Shah ruled from 1719 to 1748 A.D. [37-006] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): Uttarakhand P.C.S. (Pre) 2002
Q: Arrange the following four foreign invasions in chronological order and find the correct answer from the codes given below:
   | 1. Ahmad Shah Abdali | 2. Genghis Khan |
   | 3. Nadir Shah | 4. Timur |
   Code:
Options: (a) 1, 2, 3, 4 (b) 4, 3, 2, 1 (c) 2, 4, 3, 1 (d) 2, 4, 1, 3
Answer: (c) 2, 4, 3, 1
Explanation: The correct sequence of the foreign invaders is Genghis Khan, Timur, Nadir Shah and Ahmad Shah Abdali. Genghis Khan attacked during the reign of Iltutmish (1211-1236 A.D.), Timur invaded during the reign of Nasiruddin Mahmud in 1398 AD. Nadir Shah invaded during the reign of Muhammad Shah in 1739 A.D. and the year of the invasion of Ahmad Shah Abdali was 1748-69 A.D.

[37-007] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.S.C (GIC) 2010
Q: Which of the following statements are correct?
   1. Ahmad Shah Abdali fought the third battle of Panipat.
   2. Babur established the Delhi Sultanate.
   3. Shah Jahan fought the war of succession.
   4. Jahangir was a lover of beauty and nature.
   Code:
Options: (a) 1, 2 and 3 are correct (b) 1 and 3 are correct (c) 1 and 4 are correct (d) 1, 2, 3 and 4 are correct
Answer: (c) 1 and 4 are correct
Explanation: The third battle of Panipat was fought between Ahmad Shah Abdali and Marathas in 1761 A.D. Qutbuddin Aybak was the first ruler of the Delhi Sultanate and also the founder of the Slave dynasty. Shah Jahan did not fight the war of succession while it was fought among his sons (Shah Shuja, Dara Shikoh, and Aurangzeb) and Jahangir was a lover of beauty and nature.

Options: (a) Battle of Haldighati – Maharana Pratap and Akbar (b) First Battle of Panipat – Babur and Ibrahim Lodi (c) Second Battle of Panipat – Tipu Sultan and Marathas (d) More than one of the above (e) None of the above
Answer: (c) Second Battle of Panipat – Tipu Sultan and Marathas
Explanation: Battle of Haldighati was fought on 18 June 1576 between forces of Mewar led by Maharana Pratap and the forces of Mughals (Akbar) led by Man Singh I of Amber. / First Battle of Panipat (1526) was fought between invading forces of Babur and Ibrahim Lodi of Lodi Empire. / Second Battle of Panipat (1556) was fought between King of Delhi Hem Chandra Vikramaditya, popularly known as Hemu and army of Akbar.

[37-024] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 2 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2001; 69th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 2023

Answer: (d) Surat

[37-026] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2001
Q: Mughals adopted “Nawruj” festival from –
Options: (a) Zoroastrians (b) Jews (c) Mongols (d) Turks
Answer: (a) Zoroastrians
Explanation: Nawruz festival was adopted from Persia (Iran). It was a national festival which was celebrated for nineteen days. It had great significance during the Mughal period. The preparation of the festival used to begin quite long before. Abul Qadir Badauni stated Nawruz as “Nawruz-i-Jalali.”

[37-027] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2016
Q: During the Mughal period, the Madarsa, which was specialized in the teaching of ‘Muslim Jurisprudence,’ was located in –
Options: (a) Lucknow (b) Delhi (c) Sialkot (d) Hyderabad (India)
Answer: (a) Lucknow
Explanation: The teaching of ‘Muslim Jurisprudence’ was specialized in Lucknow Madrasa during Mughal period. Mulla Nizamuddin born in Sihali, (Lucknow) was the originator of ‘Darse Nizami,’ the new curriculum which included Tafsir (exegesis of Quran), Hadith (tradition of Prophet Muhammad) and Fiqh (Islamic Jurisprudence).

[37-028] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2024
Q: Consider the following events and arrange them in chronological order:
   1. Murder of Abul Fazl
   2. Death of Sheikh Mubarak
   3. Death of Faizi
   4. Death of Daniyal
   Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
Options: (a) 2, 1, 4, 3 (b) 3, 2, 1, 4 (c) 2, 3, 1, 4 (d) 4, 3, 1, 2
Answer: (c) 2, 3, 1, 4
Explanation: Sheikh Mubarak, who was the father of Abul Fazl and Faizi, passed away in 1593 CE. Faizi died in 1595 CE. The assassination of Abul Fazl occurred in 1602 CE on the orders of Prince Salim. Akbar's son Daniyal passed away in 1605 CE. Thus, the correct chronological order is 2, 3, 1, 4.

[37-030] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2010
Q: Which one of the following is correctly matched?
Options: (a) Khuldabad - Tomb of Shaikh Salim Chishti (b) Fatehpur Sikri - Tomb of Itmad-ud-Daula (c) Agra - Tomb of Aurangzeb (d) Delhi - Tomb of Abdur Rahim Khan-i-Khanan
Answer: (d) Delhi - Tomb of Abdur Rahim Khan-i-Khanan
Explanation: The famous Dargah or Tomb of Shaikh Salim Chishti is located in Fatehpur Sikri. Tomb of Itmad-ud-Daula is located in Agra and the tomb of Aurangzeb is situated at Khuldabad (Chhatrapati Sambhaji Nagar). The tomb of Abdur Rahim Khan-i-Khanan is situated in Delhi. Therefore, option (d) is correctly matched.

Q: With reference to the cultural history of India, consider the following statements:

Its roofis built using just bricks and without beams or garters. … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

Q: During the Mughal period ‘Muallim ‘ was –
Options: (a) A tax (b) An administrative unit (c) A ruler (d) A ship
Answer: *
Explanation: Muallim or navigator was an employee on Indian merchant ships. He used to hold a spherical device on the ship to identify the position of stars and the sun. It helped him to identify the correct position of his ship. In the given options, option (d) seems to be near to the answer, but it is not an appropriate answer as Muallim was an employee/worker, not a ship.

[37-040] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2016
Q: Banjaras during the medieval period of Indian history were generally
Options: (a) Agriculturists (b) Warriors (c) Weavers (d) Traders
Answer: (d) Traders
Explanation: Banjaras during the medieval period of Indian history were traders. They used to move from one place to another, sometimes with thousands of oxen, laden with food grain, salt, ghee and other daily stuff. They were specialized in Caravan trading and also in textile embroidery, etc.

[37-042] Ch37 · Mughal Period : Miscellaneous · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2019
Q: Which of the following is NOT correctly matched?
Options: (a) Dam Copper coin (b) Desai Revenue Collector (c) Diwan Revenue Chiefofa province (d) Jarib A type of tax
Answer: (d) Jarib A type of tax
Explanation: Shershah Suri promulgated copper coin 'Dam'. During Mughal period one Rupiyah (silver coin) was equal to 40 dam. / Desai was a revenue collector at the district level during Maratha period. / Diwan was revenue chief of a province in Mughal period. Jarib was a land measurement unit during Mughal period. Hence, (d) is not correctly matched.

   Assertion (A): Shivaji had to face opposition from big Deshmukhs.
   Reason (R): These Deshmukhs were not in support of the independent Maratha state and wanted to remain as feudal lords of Bijapur.
   Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A). (b) (A) is false, but (R) is true. (c) Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A). (d) (A) is true, but (R) is false.
Answer: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A).
Explanation: Shivaji had to face opposition from powerful landlords (Deshmukhs) because the landlords wanted to maintain their power and influence. Hence, statement (A) is true. / The Deshmukhs were not in favour of an independent Maratha state and wanted to remain subordinates of Bijapur, as remaining under Bijapur allowed them autonomy and opportunities to maintain their special privileges. This is why they opposed Shivaji. … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

[39-024] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.R.O./ A.R.O. (Mains) 2016
Q: Which of the following coins was NOT a silver coin in the Kingdom of Chhatrapati Shivaji?
Options: (a) Rupaya (b) Lari (c) Taka (d) Ruka
Answer: (d) Ruka
Explanation: Lari, Rupaya & Taka were silver coins during the reign of Chhatrapati Shivaji, while Ruka was a coin in Kakatiya dynasty.

[39-026] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Spl) (Mains) 2004
Q: Arrange the following in the chronological order of their rule:
   (1) Bajirao
   (2) Balaji Bajirao
   (3) Balaji Vishwanath
   (4) Madhavrao
   Select the correct answer using the codes given below.
   Code:
Options: (a) 4, 3, 1, 2 (b) 1, 2, 3, 4 (c) 3, 1, 2, 4 (d) 2, 1, 4, 3
Answer: (c) 3, 1, 2, 4
Explanation: Balaji Vishwanath served as Peshwa from 1713 A.D. to 1720 A.D.. Shahu appointed the elder son of Balaji Vishwanath Bajirao-I as Peshwa whose tenure was from 1720 to 1740 A.D.. Balaji Bajirao served as Peshwa from 1740 to 1761 A.D. after the death of Bajirao. Madhavrao’s tenure was from 1761 to 1772 A.D..

Options: (a) I, II, IV, III (b) II, I, IV, III (c) I, II, III, IV (d) I, III, II, IV
Answer: (a) I, II, IV, III
Explanation: | Balaji Vishwanath | (1713-1720) | / | Bajirao I | (1720-1740) | / | Madhav Rao I | (1761-1772) | / | Narayan Rao | (1772-1773) | / Hence option (a) is correct answer.

[39-029] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. P.C.S. (Pre) 2024
Q: Consider the following Peshwas and arrange them in chronological order:
   1. Raghunath Rao (Raghoba)
   2. Balaji Bajirao
   3. Narayan Rao
   4. Balaji Vishwanath
   Choose the correct answer from the options given below:
Options: (a) 4, 2, 3, 1 (b) 3, 4, 1, 2 (c) 1, 2, 3, 4 (d) 1, 3, 2, 4
Answer: (a) 4, 2, 3, 1
Explanation: | Peshwa | Tenure | / | Balaji Vishwanath | 1713-20 CE | / | Balaji Bajirao | 1740-61 CE | / | Narayan Rao | 1772-73 CE | / | Raghunath Rao (Raghoba) | 1773-74 CE | / Thus, the correct chronological order will be: / Balaji Vishwanath → Balaji Bajirao → Narayan Rao → Raghunath Rao (Raghoba)

[39-031] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 1997; U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2007
Q: Assertion (A): By 1750 the Maratha kingdom had become a Confederacy presided over by the Peshwa.
   Reason (R): The successors of Shahu depended on the will of the Peshwa.
   Code:
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A). (b) Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A). (c) (A) is true, but (R) is false. (d) (A) is false, but (R) is true.
Answer: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A).
Also asked: The Maratha King became a non-entity and the Peshwa, the virtual ruler from the time of – -> Balaji Bajirao
Explanation: Shahu appointed Balaji Bajirao as Peshwa after the death of Bajirao in 1740 A.D.. Now, the designation of Peshwa became hereditary. By the Sangola agreement of 1750 A.D., the Peshwa emerged as the real and effective head of Maratha Confederacy.

[39-033] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.R.O./ A.R.O. (Mains) 2016
Q: Given below are two statements, one is labelled as Assertion (A) and other as Reason (R).
   Assertion (A): Marathas emerged as the strongest na tive power in India after the decline of Mughal Empire.
   Reason (R): Marathas were the first rulers who had a clear concept of United Indian Nation.
   Choose the correct answer from the code given below.
   Code:
Options: (a) Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A) (b) Both (A) and (R) are true but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A) (c) (A) is true but (R) is false (d) (A) is false but (R) is true
Answer: (c) (A) is true but (R) is false
Explanation: After the decline of Mughals, Marathas were the most capable native power who could have taken the place of Mughals. Rise of Maratha power was based on people of Maharashtra and they were united as a nation on the basis of Maratha identity. However Maratha's failed to develop a concept of unified India which is a major criticism against them. Hence statement (A) is correct but (R) is false.

[39-034] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 2 paper(s): U.P. R.O./A.R.O (Mains) 2013; U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2012
Q: At the time of Aurangzeb’s death, the Maratha leadership was in the hands of?
Options: (a) Sambhaji (b) Rajaram (c) Jijabai (d) Tarabai
Answer: (d) Tarabai
Explanation: Rajaram led Marathas as a representative of Shahu from 1689 to 1700 A.D.. After the death of Rajaram, his widow Tarabai struggled with the Mughals on behalf of her son Shivaji-II. At the time of Aurangzeb’s death, the Maratha leadership was in the hands of Tarabai.

[39-035] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Spl) (Pre) 2008
Q: Who among the following Maratha women led struggles against the Mughal Empire from 1700 A.D. onwards?
Options: (a) Ahilya Bai (b) Mukta Bai (c) Tara Bai (d) Rukmini Bai
Answer: (c) Tara Bai
Explanation: After the death of Rajaram, his minor son Shivaji-II became king and widow Tarabai became his regent. Tarabai was a brave lady. She led the struggle against the Mughals and took the forts of Singhgarh, Raigarh and Satara from Mughals.

[39-036] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 1 paper(s): 39th B.P.S.C. (Pre) 1994
Q: Saranzami system was related to –
Options: (a) Land revenue system of Maratha (b) Taluqdari system (c) Qutb Shahi administration (d) None of the above
Answer: (a) Land revenue system of Maratha
Explanation: Saranzami system was related to feudal-military land grant system in the Maratha period. The land given under Saranzami system was provided for the livelihood of Maratha Jagirdars (for maintenance of forts/troops for public service) during the Maratha period.

[39-037] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2018
Q: The Maratha claim of revenue for protection is known by what name?
Options: (a) Sardesh Mukhi (b) Chauth (c) Abwab (d) Jamadani
Answer: (b) Chauth
Explanation: Chauth was the revenue for protection claimed by Marathas. It was collected from the provinces which were known as Mughalai.

[39-038] Ch39 · Maratha State and Confederacy · asked in 1 paper(s): Chhattisgarh P.C.S. (Pre) 2003
Q: A historian witness to the third battle of Panipat. Who was he?
Options: (a) Khafi Khan (b) Kashiraj Pandit (c) Dattaji Pingle (d) Harcharan Das
Answer: (b) Kashiraj Pandit
Explanation: The third battle of Panipat was fought between Maratha Sadashivrao Bhau and Afghan invader Ahmad Shah Abdali on 14th January, 1761. Marathas were badly defeated by Afghans in this battle. According to Kashiraj Pandit, a historian who was an eyewitness of this battle "The Third Battle of Panipat became catastrophic for Marathas".

Explanation: The immediate reason for the third battle of Panipat was the Ahmad Shah Abdali’s desire to avenge Marathas as they expelled his viceroy Timur Shah from Lahore.

[40-002] Ch40 · Disintegration of the Mughal Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre) 2003
Q: How did the Mughal Emperor Jahandar Shah’s reign come to an early end?
Options: (a) He was deposed by his Wazir (b) He died due to slip while climbing down steps (c) He was defeated by his nephew in a battle (d) He died ofsickness due to too much consumption of wine.
Answer: (c) He was defeated by his nephew in a battle
Explanation: Jahandar Shah was the first Mughal emperor who proved to be unfit for governance. He ascended the throne with the help of contemporary, powerful Amir Zulfiqar Khan. He was defeated by his nephew, Farrukh Siyar and killed on Feb, 1713. People called him ‘’lewd Moron’’.

[40-003] Ch40 · Disintegration of the Mughal Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.S.C. (GIC) 2010
Q: Which of the following Mughal Emperors granted duty-free trading facilities to the English in Bengal?
Options: (a) Akbar (b) Jahangir (c) Bahadur Shah (d) Farrukh Siyar
Answer: (d) Farrukh Siyar
Explanation: Farrukh Siyar issued a decree in which he granted duty-free trading facilities to the Britishers in Bengal in 1717 A.D. It was mentioned in the contract that the East India Company could trade without duty (charge) after paying the annual tax of 3000.

They were leaders of Hindustani group. They were famous as 'King Makers'. They made Farrukh Siyar king in 1713 AD and dethroned him in 1719 AD. After that puppet king Rafi-ud-darjat, Rafi-ud-daula and Muhammad Shah sat on throne with their help. … [shortened in this brief; the book continues]

Answer: (b) Muhammad Shah
Also asked: The Mughal Emperor at the time of Nadir Shah’s attack was – -> Muhammad Shah
Explanation: Muhammad Shah (1719 - 1748 A.D.) was the last Mughal emperor to sit on Mayur Singhasan/ Takht-i-Taus during his reign. Nadir Shah attacked India in 1739 A.D. and defeated Mughals in Karnal Battle. The contemporary historian Anand Ram Mukhlis wrote, "Nadir Shah took sixty thousand rupees, many thousands of gold coins, gold worth of one crore rupees,jewels offifty crores, Kohinoor and peacock throne with him to Iran".

[40-007] Ch40 · Disintegration of the Mughal Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): M.P.P.C.S. (Pre) 2023
Q: In which of the following year did Nadir Shah invade India?
Options: (a) 1736 A.D. (b) 1737 A.D. (c) 1738 A.D. (d) 1739 A.D.
Answer: (d) 1739 A.D.
Explanation: Nadir Shah invaded India in the year 1739 AD. The invasion culminated in the sack of Delhi, during which the city suffered widespread destruction, looting and the loss of many lives. This event is known as the Battle of Karnal and it had significant repercussions for the Mughal Empire, contributing to its decline and the subsequent rise of regional powers in India.

[40-015] Ch40 · Disintegration of the Mughal Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2006
Q: Which one of the following statements is not correct about Bahadur Shah Zafar?

Answer: (d) He received less than one lakh rupees as pension from East India Company
Explanation: Bahadur Shah Zafar received one lakh rupees per month as a pension, which consisted of Rs. 99000 given in Delhi and one thousand rupees for regular family expenses in Lucknow. So, option (d) is not correct, but other statements are correct.

[40-017] Ch40 · Disintegration of the Mughal Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Spl) (Mains) 2008
Q: Who among the following was the first Nawab of Oudh?
Options: (a) Asaf-ud-Daula (b) Saadat Khan (c) Shuja-ud-Daula (d) Safdarjung
Answer: (b) Saadat Khan
Explanation: The first Nawab of Oudh was Saadat Khan. Turanis established Hyderabad and Iranis established Oudh. In 1722 A.D., Saadat Khan was appointed as the governor of Oudh where he governed through his heir nephew Safdarjung and played high politics in Awadh royal court. After his death, Safdarjung refused to give nominal loyalty to the Mughal Emperor.

[40-018] Ch40 · Disintegration of the Mughal Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P. R.O./A.R.O. (Pre) 2023
Q: With reference to the Burhan-ul-Mulk Saadat Khan, which of the following statements is/are correct?
   1. Saadat Khan was the founder of Independent state of Awadh.
   2. He took part in the conspiracy against the Sayyid Brothers.
   Select the correct answer from the code given below:
   Code -
Options: (a) Only 2 (b) Both 1 and 2 (c) Neither 1 nor 2 (d) Only 1
Answer: (b) Both 1 and 2
Explanation: The independent state of Awadh was formed during reign of Mughal emperor Muhammad Shah. The founder of this state was Saadat Khan. Saadat Khan had taken the side of emperor Muhammad Shah in the conspiracy hatched against the Sayyid brothers. Pleased with his work, Muhammad Shah gave him the title of Burhan-ul-Mulk. Hence both statements are correct.

[40-019] Ch40 · Disintegration of the Mughal Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): U.P.P.C.S. (Mains) 2011
Q: Who among the following was the founder of independent Kingdom of Hyderabad?
Options: (a) Qamaruddin Khan (b) Muhammad Amin Khan (c) Asad Khan (d) Chin Qilich Khan
Answer: (d) Chin Qilich Khan
Explanation: Chin Qilich Khan aka Nizam-ul-Mulk founded the independent kingdom of Hyderabad in 1724 A.D. Nizam became the real king after the death of the Mughal governor Mubarij Khan in the battle of Sakhar-Kherda of Deccan, 1724 A.D.

   (i) Kilich Khan was also known as Mubariz Khan.
   (ii) Nizam-ul-Mulk defeated Mubariz Khan in Battle of Shakar Kheda.
   (iii) Julfikar Khan gave Nizam-ul-Mulk with the title of Asaf-Jah.
Options: (a) Only (i) (b) (i) and (iii) (c) (ii) and (iii) (d) (i), (ii) and (iii)
Answer: *
Explanation: Qilich Khan was called as Nizam-ul-Mulk Asaf Jah. The Battle of Sakhar Kherda was held in 1724 AD between Nizam-ul-Mulk and Mubariz Khan. He defeated and killed Mubariz Khan. Mughal emperor Mohammad Shah gave Nizam-ul-Mulk the title of Asaf-Jah.

[40-021] Ch40 · Disintegration of the Mughal Empire · asked in 1 paper(s): I.A.S. (Pre.) 2021
Q: With reference to Indian history, which of the following statements is/are correct?
   1. The Nizamat of Arcot emerged out of Hyderabad state.
   2. The Mysore Kingdom emerged out of Vijayanagara Empire.
   3. Rohilkhand Kingdom was formed out of the territories occupied by Ahmad Shah Durrani.
   Select the correct answer using the code given below.
Options: (a) 1 and 2 (b) 2 only (c) 2 and 3 (d) 3 only
Answer: (b) 2 only
Explanation: Nizamat of Arcot was established by Mughal Emperor Aurangzeb and was the legal dependency of the Nizam of Hyderabad, it did not emerge from the Hyderabad state. Hence, statement 1 is not correct. Mysore kingdom emerged as a powerful state under the Wodeyar dynasty, which initially served as a vassal of the Vijayanagar empire. Hence statement 2 is correct.
